Camden, 2026-09-07: no attachment to the IMU's position — "I would like the wires to look as simple as possible: sensor / input to one side, output to gauges / digital display and CAN on the other" — and V-057 "verified available". The enclosure he described in the same answer — a very condensed protective case with the connector halves embedded in it, easy to remove and refit — is Q-126, because it changes two housings' halves and the Deutsch count.

D-266 — The wideband's AEMnet frame is confirmed from AEM's own 30-0300 sheet (01-REFERENCE/AEM_AFR_manual.pdf): CAN 2.0B, 500 kbit/s, 29-bit identifier 0x00000180 (menu "ID 1"; ID 2–16 are 0x181–0x18F), 100 Hz, DLC 8, big-endian; bytes 0–1 lambda u16 × 0.0001, 2–3 oxygen s16 × 0.001 %, 4 system volts u8 × 0.1 V, 5 reserved, byte 6 flags (bit 1 LSU4.9 detected · bit 5 free-air cal in use · bit 7 lambda data valid), byte 7 bit 6 sensor fault; no internal termination. 2026-09-07, closing Q-304. Consequences: the luxury CAN map's AEM row goes from vendor — unconfirmed to confirmed and carries a rate, so the bus-load table includes it (~3 % at 100 Hz); the ICU's receive filter and the PMU's CAN-input definition must both accept an extended identifier and swap byte order for this one frame; the gauge adds no terminator, so D-079's two stand; a valid AFR is the lambda field and byte 6 bit 7 — the ICU renders dashes when the valid bit is clear or the fault bit is set, never a stale number (luxury D-153). Supply: 1.5 A nominal, 3.0 A peak at 13.8 V, minimum 10 V — F20 at 7.5 A stands, and the gauge showing "bAtt" during a cold crank is expected, not a fault.

D-269 — The display chain is chain (ii): a 900–1000-nit 1920 × 720 bar panel through its scaler board, bridged from the BT817 by a TFP410-class HDMI encoder, the canvas scaled 1.5×; the binnacle plate stays as the bezel's sub-frame (10 mm flange, its holes the ones the bezel keeps); the odometer is stored in non-volatile memory no matter what — the Teensy's wear-levelled EEPROM emulation as the authoritative copy, written every 0.1 mile, and the microSD as the log — and its persistence across a power cycle is a gate for the meters cutover. Camden's three answers, 2026-09-08: V-085 "(ii) for sure — I do not want to depend on the shade from the dash when the sun glints at a funny angle"; Q-130 and Q-131 "follow recommendations", with the odometer to be "saved to non-volatile memory no matter what". The brow question of M-1 no longer decides the glass, only the plate's dimensions; V-084 (pixel clock and sync polarity) is agent work against the chosen panel at install §1.22; T-051 (the BT817 evaluation board, P117) is ordered now. Parts P118 / P119 carry the chain-ii specification; the EEPROM copy means a missing or corrupt card can never lose the mileage (luxury F-007 gains that clause).

D-270 — The ICU lives in a protected enclosure with its connector halves built into the wall: two Deutsch DT13 flanged PCB receptacles (6-way power / bus, 12-way sensors) on the board, sealed through one face; the display ribbon and the page button through grommets in the opposite face; an RF-transparent wall in front of the radio's antenna. The harness side of DP-ICU-A / DP-ICU-B becomes a DT06 plug on a 150 mm tail from the dash post, so the ICU plugs in like any bought module. The enclosure is 3D-printed in ASA or PETG (never PLA — a dash reaches 70 °C in the sun) around the board, with printed bosses for the DT13 flanges and a gasket groove in the lid; a polycarbonate Hammond 1554-series box is the fallback if printing disappoints. Camden's call, 2026-09-08, answering Q-126: "keep the ICU in a protected container … ideally the connectors are embedded and the harness just plugs in like any other bought part" — and his question, one connector like the PMU or two: two, because the whole harness is already tooled for DT (a single 18-way would mean an HD30 / DRC family, new contacts, new tools, a bigger flange), because the 6-way is the module pattern every drop shares (D-252) and the 12-way keeps sensors on their own housing, and because "out" is only the display — CAN is on the 6-way both ways, so nothing else leaves the box. Consequences: DP-ICU-A / DP-ICU-B box_side become DT13-06PA / DT13-12PA (P122); the two DT04 receptacle kits leave the Deutsch count (P099, P115 re-drawn) and the two post clips with them (P030 → 21); V-073's edge rule (D-265) is the enclosure's face rule. Printing is at CSU's makerspace if it has one, else any print service — the file is ours either way.

D-273 — The ICU carrier's parts check (V-082) is done: five values change, the rest stand. Agent, 2026-09-08, against the datasheets, as V-082 asked (D-265). Changes: (1) the logic buck is an LMR36015-Q1-class part (60 V in, 1.5 A) — the LMR33630's 36 V absolute maximum sits under the SMBJ33A's ~53 V clamping voltage, so a load dump the clamp lets through would take the buck; (2) the backlight switch is a ≥ 60 V device for the same reason — an SQD50P06-class P-FET or a VN5E-class high-side driver rated ≥ 6 A — behind a 4 A fuse, because the 900–1000-nit bar and its scaler draw ~2.5 A at 12 V (D-269), not the 2 A the 3 A fuse assumed; (3) the LM393 runs from 5 V with 10 kΩ pull-ups to 3V3 — its common-mode input range stops 1.5 V below its supply, so on 3.3 V the tach stage's 5.1 V zener node would clip; (4) the IMU is an LSM6DSO or ICM-42688-P — the MPU-6050 and ICM-20948 are not recommended for new designs; (5) the ESP32-C3 sits on its own 3.3 V LDO (the XIAO module's) — a BLE scan peaks near 300 mA and the Teensy's regulator is a 250 mA part. Confirmed as drawn: SS34 and SMBJ33A at each 12 V input, the BAT54S clamps, BZX84C5V1, the 100 Ω 1 W excitations (D-260), the 15 kΩ / 10 kΩ and 47 kΩ / 10 kΩ dividers, TCAN1042HVDRQ1 (D-265), APS6404L-3SQR PSRAM, the DT13-06PA / -12PA receptacles (D-270; the flange-gasket suffix is confirmed at order). The 5 V rail budget is ~0.8 A peak. The same buck finding applies to the DCU's two bucks (luxury LP08, V-083). Data: P105, P107, P109, P116; IC04, IC12, IC13, IC23, IC24; ICU-CARRIER.md. Closes V-082.

D-271 — Reserves live at the post, never in the engine bay. The engine leg carries only what the 12A on the mounts needs plus the CAN2 terminator drop; every future-engine provision is pinned on the PMU side of the L1 receptacles and sealed on the leg side. A new cavity state, RESERVED, says exactly that: L1-P 2 / L1-P 3 (O13 / O14, 12 AWG from the pins to the receptacle cavities), L1-S2 9 (the spare block-B position wired to its cavity, no fuse) and L1-S2 11 (wired across the node to the ICU's spare input DP-ICU-B 9) are RESERVED; L1-S2 4 · 5 · 6 become sealing plugs — a sensor spare with no destination at the post is pinned at the swap, when it has one. The same rule for the chassis legs: a branch with no device ends at the last connector of the main leg, not at a capped wire near where a device might one day be (Q-132 applies it to the three existing stubs). Camden's call, 2026-09-08, answering Q-127 in his own words: "wire the PMU, ICU and DCU all in one go, supporting any engine … the chassis legs go fully wired, but branches with no end are just a connector and the main leg; the engine leg is the unique one — completely clean for the current 12A, connecting to the PMU side via a connector with many unpinned spots, so the PMU side can be fully pinned for as many engines and transmissions as possible, but I don't have to wire them past the PMU / fuses / relays." This is D-211(d) made precise: the interface to any future engine is the L1 receptacles at the dash post, pinned; the leg itself is disposable. What leaves the carts: about 110 ft of wire and eight leg-side contacts; what joins: one sealing plug per RESERVED cavity on the leg side (the size-12 and size-16 plug lines are re-counted). tools/rx7.py learned the state: the cut list prints a RESERVED cavity as "no leg conductor", the sealing-plug counts take one half per RESERVED cavity, and a pin's destinations read "reserved at the post". Supersedes D-007's "capped at the bulkhead" for O13 / O14 and the three sensor spares, and D-250's leg-side conductors for L1-S2 9 and 11; D-252's tunnel pair L4-S 3 / 4 is untouched — it is a chassis leg and now has a device (Q-309).

D-272 — Road speed is a Hall pulse generator on the transmission's speedo-drive boss, the cable deleted; the tunnel pair goes LIVE at the meters cutover. Camden, 2026-09-08, on Q-309: "Follow recommendations assuming many transmissions will be able to support the same type of system even if a different sensor at the trans is needed. but if the transmission base dies when the jatco trans does its not the path." The condition holds: the sensor is a bought part on a standard speedo-drive thread, so the same kind of sensor fits whatever transmission follows the Jatco if it has a drive, and if it has none (the CD009) speed arrives on the same ICU channel from the ECU's VSS frame (engine-swap HO15, luxury F-014) — the approach outlives this transmission either way. Data: L4-S 3 (signal) and L4-S 4 (supply) are LIVE; the supply is an O10 tap (N70) so the sensor lives and dies with the ICU, and the block-B spare position stays the phase-2 sensor supply for L1-S2 9 alone (N68); DP-ICU-B 8 LIVE, IC08; DV47, G35; P113 re-specified (Dakota Digital SEN-01-4160 / AutoMeter 5291 class, 8 000 or 16 000 pulses per mile), bought after M-2 measures the boss; MG22 carries it. Pulses per mile is a config value, trued once against a measured mile. Closes Q-309; the pending audit's Q-115 (road speed) is answered by this.

D-274 — The chassis principle: a future circuit's conductor ends in a dust-capped receptacle at the point it leaves its leg, never as a capped wire where the device will be; the two release solenoids are the exception because they exist, and are wired to; the door receptacles are RESERVED at the sill. Camden, 2026-09-08, on Q-132: "wire both release solenoids all the way to the part. both solenoids actual exist and i believe work. the heated wiper spray will no be run all the way to the part just into the front chassis leg and into half of a connector pair, then i run the rest and plug in when the part is here. verify that other chasis future circuits follow the same principle (ends at a connector at the branch off each main leg)." The verification: seven provisions ran past their branch point as bare capped wire; each now ends in a receptacle there, the L3-BLW pattern (D-253), and the plug half of each pair waits on the shelf for the part. L2-NZL (DT-2, cowl) for the nozzle feed L2-S 6; L2-OAT (DT-2, nose) for the outside-air thermistor L2-S 5; L3-CMF (DTP-2, behind the centre stack) for the O15 comfort bus L3-P 2 — the luxury package's fuse block plugs in; L3-WIN (DT-6, console) for the four window commands L3-S2 3–6 plus a switch-common feed tapped off the F3 switch supply where L3-S2 2 passes; L3-MOD (DT-2, behind the dash) for the future module L3-S2 7 / 8; L3-RDR and L4-RDR (DT-4, behind the dash and at the rear node) for the radar pass-through L3-S3 1–3 ↔ L4-S 5–7 — the run up the hatch goes in with the sensor. The window commands L4-M 9–12 already end at the sill node's relay sockets, which is their branch point. The doors: the door harness is the mirror's and the window's job, so D1 / D2 are RESERVED (D-271's state) — pinned on the node side at the sill, sealing plugs in the door plugs; nothing crosses a hinge boot until there is a part on the other side. The solenoids: L4-M 3 runs through the hinge loop to the hatch-release solenoid and L4-M 4 to the fuel-door solenoid — both factory parts, present and believed working (K-017 said the fuel-door one never existed; corrected) — their post ends capped until the luxury package fits the trigger (D-180: the K3 / K4 sockets are the slot). The engine bay is untouched: the L1 leg's reserves are D-271's, at the post. Data: seven housings and twenty-two cavities (ten of them plugs), fourteen door cavities RESERVED, DV48 / DV49, parts P030 (28 clips), P035, P036, P099, P100, P102, P028 / P029 (the plug counts), new P123–P125; views.py — the new housing codes join the cut-list groups and the cavity-reference patterns, and the dangling-reference check now also reads the two-letter drop suffixes (DP-ICU-A n). Luxury D-315 mirrors it. Closes Q-132.

D-268 — The digital display is this build's, installed with the ICU: there is no temporary cluster, no CLU pigtail and no gauge drive. The factory cluster stays alive on the factory harness until the meters cutover (MG22), which is gated on the display proven on the bench, and leaves with that harness at install §6. The display chain — the BT817 evaluation board (T-051), the glass (V-085), the timings (V-084) and parts P117–P120 — moves into this project; road speed (Q-309) is fitted at the cutover; the odometer starts from the factory cluster's reading and persists to microSD (luxury F-007, a cutover gate); the display mounts on a plain plate in the binnacle aperture (P121) and the moulded bezel around it is the luxury package's (luxury FT34, D-314). Camden's call, 2026-09-07, answering Q-129: install the display now and leave only the dash plastics — a foam-and-fibreglass mould around the display — to the luxury package; wiring for one instrument is cleaner than wiring for two. The reason it costs nothing in safety is one the design already had: the parallel-system migration (D-023 / D-024) keeps the factory cluster on the factory harness until its own circuit's cutover, so the fallback D-258 built a pigtail for exists without a wire. What leaves the record: DV32, channels IC17–IC20, the CLU header, install §1.22–1.23 as they were, the cluster letters of T-017 sheet C, and D-263 — withdrawn as moot, the gauges' construction no longer matters. What it pulls forward: the display chain's bench work (weeks, in parallel with a harness build that takes months), a pulse generator before the first drive without the cluster, and persistence for the odometer. Amends D-258 (the cluster's temporary role and everything built for it; the topology — senders terminate at the ICU, no cluster drop, the PMU-excited oil node — stands) and D-259 ("only the display comes with luxury" → only the bezel does).

D-267 — The ICU carries a radio co-processor: an ESP32-C3 module on the carrier's 3.3 V rail, on a UART to the Teensy, decoding battery data over Bluetooth LE and republishing it as a CAN frame (0x220, 2 Hz — volts, amps, state of charge, temperature, flags) that the display draws and the PMU logs. Nothing at the drops or in the harness changes; the enclosure wall in front of its antenna must be RF-transparent, or the antenna goes outside (a constraint on Q-126). A dev board is bought now to try the decode (P116); which source it decodes — the Ionic's BMS, a shunt, or both — is Q-128. Camden's call, 2026-09-07: "the board is cheap and worth buying to try; if it is an isolated addition inside the ICU case leaving everything the same, then definitely plan to add it." It is: channel IC24, two UART lines and two GPIOs on the board, ~120 mA on a rail budgeted at 0.6 A, no cavity, no conductor, no cart line beyond the module. The Teensy stays the ICU's brain (the reasoning is in Q-128: the ESP32's ADC, its render bandwidth, the Teensy-shaped firmware, three Teensys in hand); the radio's boot time and its FreeRTOS timing never touch the instrument core. Because the ICU's logic rail is O10 (accessory), the radio runs only with the key in — the overnight consumption is read at key-on from the BMS's or the shunt's own counters, which is what G4 needs. D-251 holds: the frame is telemetry; state of charge may enter the 11.5 V shed rule only as a secondary, fail-open term. The frame's layout is the luxury CAN map's (0x220); the firmware work is luxury F-015.

D-262 — The sensor inputs are built to outlive the 12A. Every ICU sender channel is a pull-up behind a jumper (fitted for the 12A's 2-wire resistive senders, open for a 0–5 V transducer), a ×0.4 divider that reads either, and a per-channel curve in config-as-data — never a board change; the PMU's A7 takes a resistive sender with the 100 Ω pull-up in (D-260) or a 0–5 V pressure transducer with it lifted at N39; the engine leg is cut at the grommet and rebuilt at the swap (D-211(d)), and what the new engine must deliver to these inputs is the hand-over table in ../engine-swap/README.md. Camden, 2026-09-07: "with the engine swap I will need to power the more complicated sensors" — and the bay stays stripped, so nothing is pre-wired for a sensor that does not exist. The 12A's senders are one-wire resistive parts the ICU excites; an LS's are 3-wire 5 V devices read by its own ECU, which publishes them on CAN2 (0x500+, the luxury map). So at the swap most ICU channels go quiet and the display reads the ECU's frames — a firmware rule, luxury F-014: local input when configured, else the ECU's frame, else dashes (luxury D-153) — while the two signals the car acts on stay on wire per D-251: oil pressure into A7 from a sender or transducer on the new block (never the ECU's CAN value), and the tach into DP-ICU-B 4 from the ECU's tach output through the same clamp. L1-S2 9 (switched 12 V, D-250) powers a 12 V-supplied 3-wire transducer if one is fitted; a 5 V sensor is the ECU's, or gets a regulator at the dash node — no +5 V goes down the leg (D-215). The three reserves L1-S2 4–6 and the spare L1-S2 11 → DP-ICU-B 9 are the only new-sensor conductors, and they already exist.

D-264 — The 12A's electrical sensors are the three senders and nothing else. The cut B-12 water-temperature switch stays in the thermostat housing as the port's plug, unconnected; the oil level float switch (C-06) is not wired and the CLU pigtail stays at twelve lines; there is no oil-temperature sender on the 12A and no conductor is run for one — L1-S2 10 and DP-ICU-B 7 become sealing plugs, N65 and P112 leave the record, and the ICU keeps the IC07 footprint for the swap. Camden's three answers, 2026-09-07: Q-124 "verified unneeded — leave it as the plug, unconnected"; Q-125 "no need for the added warning light — I check the oil every time I get gas"; Q-308 "no oil-temp sensor while the 12A is there; prepare for after the swap, but run no unneeded wire to the engine bay". The last restates the engine-bay rule (D-211(d)) and it wins over D-250's oil-temperature conductor: the leg is rebuilt at the swap anyway, so a wire run now for a sender that will arrive with a different engine serves nothing. What the swap needs is at the dash node — a cavity (DP-ICU-B 7, plugged) and a channel (IC07, footprint) — and both stay. Whether the rest of L1's reserves follow is Q-127. Supersedes D-250's oil-temperature conductor (its sensor supply L1-S2 9 and spare L1-S2 11 stand until Q-127) and the A-010 clause that left the level senders as a future item — they are out.

D-232 — The gauge rule is set by the enable-at limit, not the channel's rating. 2026-09-03, a clarification the automated check forced: DESIGN.md §13 said "12 AWG on 25 A, 14 AWG on 15 A", and four rows already broke it on purpose — the 16 AWG high-beam-indicator tap on O3 and the three 16 AWG capped module drops on O10. A wire has to survive what its protection lets through, and the protection is the software limit: 12 AWG above 15 A, 14 AWG above 13 A, 16 AWG at 13 A and below. A 16 AWG tap off a heavier feed is allowed only where the cavity notes it as a tap, and the check flags any other case. No wire changes.

7 · Wire, labels and materials

D-212 — Family-colour solid wire, identified by printed labels; no tracers. Camden's call, 2026-09-02, after the striped-tracer scheme proved unpurchasable as cut lengths. The colour names the family (RED power · ORN 15 A outputs at 14 AWG · BLK ground · GRY analog inputs · BLU commands · PNK switch supply and +5 V · YEL/GRN CAN); the label at each end names the wire; the cavity number and the design tables are the record. Wire comes as four 16 AWG Prysmian GXL spools from Waytek (black, red, grey, blue) plus 14/12/10 AWG cuts from WireBarn and three short 16 AWG colours (pink, yellow, green). Labels are printed on a Brother PT-D220 onto TZe-FX231 flexible-ID tape, wrapped on the wire, with clear heat-shrink over them where they live under loom or in the engine bay — chosen over heat-shrink-sleeve printers as a quarter of the cost with the same permanence. Supersedes D-016 (base colour = class, tracer = circuit), D-203(c) (PT-E300 + HSe sleeves) and D-203(h) (striped GXL by the foot).

D-203(d) / D-207 — Crimpers: one IWISS solid-contact Deutsch crimper (sizes 12/16) and an open-barrel ratchet crimper for the SICMA terminals, each accepted only after three practice crimps pass the pull test (16 AWG 20 lbf · 14 AWG 34 lbf · 12 AWG 45 lbf); a hydraulic crimper for every lug. The genuine HDT-48-00 is the gold standard; the pull test is what makes a cheaper tool acceptable.

D-135 / D-220 — SICMA terminal spares come from the two spare 39-way housings in hand (54 small, 32 large), so there is no Ballenger order. Zero spares in the car's own set is why the practice crimps use the spare sets first.

D-220 — The 2026-09-02 order changes, Camden's own edits to the carts: the wink switches are the DMWD 1NO+1NC momentary 2-pack (replacing a 5-pack); the ATC fuse assortment is out — the drawer is checked and any missing value bought locally; the 12 × 24 aluminium sheet is out (D-214); a stainless star-washer kit and three clear heat-shrink packs are in; relays are bought without spares; no spare housing pairs.

D-141 / D-144 / D-147 — No bench kit: no bench PSU, no plywood mule, no test pigtail. Every risk the bench was justified by is already covered by the parallel-system architecture (D-023) and the in-car config sequence (D-194).

D-205 — Ground studs are 3/8 stainless with star washers (four: rear, front, dash, sill); the PMU stands off its panel on three M6 spacers; F2 (2 A), F19 (3 A) and F15 (7.5 A) are values cheap assortments skip — buy them by value.

8 · Build sequence

D-023 / D-024 — Parallel-system migration, least to most consequential, ending with ignition and start. The factory harness stays intact and powered until each circuit's own cutover; never both systems on one load; the car drives home at the end of every session.

D-025 / D-174 — Soft fuses are set from current measured live in the client during migration, never from an estimate; the PMU's telemetry on the new harness is the measurement, and its gap against healthy-expected draw is also the list of tired parts to replace afterwards.

D-176 — Part replacement (a slow wiper motor, a weak pump) is a separate job after the PMU is in, identified from telemetry — not clutter inside the wiring project.

D-177 / D-199 — The pop-up motor pinout comes from factory sheet E (E-03 / E-04: YG top · WR left · RY right · R bottom; YG is the cam-driven transit contact the ladders read; WR is the constant feed and is capped). Which of R / RY carries the run winding through the cam — bridged or single — is decided by the ohm check at the motor (install M-3), the one measurement the pin plan waits on.

D-143 / D-145 — The deliberate soft-fuse trip is a required step, done in the car on the first migrated circuit (the interior lamp): set the limit to 2 A, short the output, watch it trip and retry. It is the single behaviour that distinguishes this device from a fuse box and it must be met on purpose, not at speed.

D-146 — Powered verification of the dash node is replaced by continuity testing, which needs no power and catches a wrong cavity, a missed crimp, a swapped pair and a short to the panel. Powered checks happen in the car with every output disabled.

D-127 / D-137 / D-103 — Housekeeping that stands: verify the two spare housings' terminal counts on arrival; mark cavity 1 on the housing with the paint pen before the first terminal goes in (D-139); measure the pop-up motors and check the transit contacts in one sitting.

9 · The record itself

D-026 / D-043 — Working files are Markdown; IDs are permanent and never reused; a closed question is cited with its closer.

D-233 — The record is data/*.csv; every document with a table in it is rendered. Camden's call, 2026-09-03, after the eleven "downstream edits still to make" showed what hand-mirrored tables cost: one fact in one row, referenced by ID everywhere else. tools/rx7.py loads the tables, refuses to build on any contradiction (a cavity without a source, a fuse without a load, a ladder window that overlaps, a cart line below the design's count, an unknown ID) and renders DESIGN.md, GLOSSARY.md, SHOPPING-LIST.md, INSTALL.md, WIRE-TABLES.md, PMU-CONFIG-SHEET.md, README.md and VIEW.html from templates/ (the prose) and data/ (the facts). Derived facts — a pin's destinations, a housing's used count, a wire label, the Deutsch kit counts, ADC centres, totals — are computed, never typed. Generated files carry a banner and are never edited by hand; DECISIONS.md and QUESTIONS.md stay prose. Supersedes D-221 (the tables were the record and the generator was retired — that put the same row in four files) and revives the intent of the 2026-08-30 CSV convention in a shape that keeps the documents readable.

D-222 — The shopping list is the four live carts of 2026-09-02 — Waytek $455.04 · WireBarn $382.68 · DeutschConnector.com $944.08 · Amazon $1,096.22 — plus the short list of gaps in 02-SHOPPING/SHOPPING-LIST.md §8. Nothing has been purchased; every cart waits for Camden's review and payment. Supersedes D-207's cart figures and the old five-store manifest.
